"§23 · PRODUCTION PRECONDITION\nValidation & Research Program — §23\nPRODUCTION PRECONDITION · NOT PASSED\nTESTNET ONLY\nSHADOW MODE ENFORCED\nRESERVE TRADING NOT AUTHORIZED\n\nThe validation program is not an appendix to the MTQΣ design — it is a production precondition [MS §79]. The §25.5 gates have not been conducted; no performance figure may be quoted as a measured property of MTQΣ (§23.1). Until the §23.12 gates pass, any performance figure must be labeled a prior or an illustrative projection — never a measured property of the system.\n\n§23.1 · METHODOLOGY\nThe Negative Rule & Program Structure\nPerf(old) ⇏ Perf(MTQΣ)\n\nThe starting point of the methodology is a negative rule. Historical performance figures produced under the old architecture — approximately 5.21% annualized volatility and a maximum drawdown of −4.1% — must not be presented as evidence for MTQΣ, because they were calculated under the prior architecture: a fixed-quantity basket with a fixed gold percentage and a single-AMM gold price. That engine shared none of the live machinery of v2.0 — no constituency engine, no MASE multi-model ensemble, no multi-source oracle consensus, no MARP execution gates — so its statistics describe a different system answering a different question:\n\nPerf(old architecture) ⇏ Perf(MTQΣ)\nPROGRAM STRUCTURE\nbacktest §23.2\n→\nwalk-forward §23.3\n→\npurged / embargo §23.4\n→\nMonte Carlo §23.5\n→\nperturbation §23.6\n→\nselection criterion §23.7\n→\nstress suites §23.8–§23.11\n→\ngates §23.12\n\nHistorical evidence comes from the backtest (§23.2) and walk-forward validation (§23.3), both executed under the leakage-control discipline of §23.4. Synthetic evidence comes from Monte Carlo testing (§23.5). Robustness evidence from the parameter perturbation study (§23.6). Model selection is governed by the criterion of §23.7, and adversarial evidence comes from the stress suites of §23.8–§23.11, all measured through the reserve stress equation. §23.12 consolidates the gate sequence; the gate table itself is maintained in §25.5. The discipline is the same as the no-fixed-weighting principle of §3.1: evidence about the composition is an output of transparent validation — it may not be asserted in advance of the machinery that produces it.\n\n§23.5 – §23.11 · LIVE REFERENCE ENGINE\nLive §23 Stress Matrix\ncached · deterministic seeds\n\nThe TS reference engine executes the §23.5–§23.11 scenario battery — Monte Carlo families, gold and currency stress, stable-value failures, correlation collapse and parameter perturbation — on deterministic seeded generators against the live pilot state. These results exercise the reference implementation; they are necessary but not sufficient for the §23.12 gates (see the remaining-program panel below).\n\nScenario\t§\tRuns\tSurvival\tWorst min RR\tFinal status\tVerdict\n\nS1 · §23.5 Historical Block Bootstrap\n2000 runs × 90 ticks; Failing scenarios (survivalRate < 0.90): S1, S2, S3, S4, S5, S11. These are reference-engine simulation results only — NOT PRODUCTION-AUTHORIZED — validation-stage results only. No §25.5 validation gate is passed by this output.\n\nThese results are reference-engine simulations run against the MTQΣ pilot TypeScript engine with deterministic seeds and synthetic price paths. This is NOT the full §23 production research program: no 2010 historical backtest, no walk-forward validation record, and no purged/leakage-controlled out-of-sample study is included here (§23.2–§23.4 remain deferred). All §25.5 validation gates (smart-contract audit, independent model validation, Sharia certification, legal opinion, public testnet, penetration testing, institutional review, liquidity bootstrapping, governance launch, community stress test, mainnet approval) remain NOT PASSED. Nothing in this payload authorizes production use of the MTQΣ system.\n\ncached deterministic seeds · re-runs available via ?force=1 on the API. Survival = fraction of runs with reserve ratio ≥ RR_HARD; worst min RR = the lowest reserve ratio observed in any run.\n\n§23.5 · SYNTHETIC EVIDENCE\nMonte Carlo Generator Families\n7 families · {W_t^(k)} k=1..N\n\nHistorical data contains one realized path; a monetary reference must survive the paths that did not happen. Monte Carlo testing generates N simulated paths under multiple distributions and regimes, and tests the stability of the live weight trajectory W_t under path variations [MS §83]. Each family is constructed so that it attacks a different assumption of the engine, and each generator is versioned with the methodology so results are reproducible (§24.1):\n\nFamily\tGenerator\tFailure assumption attacked\nHistorical block bootstrap\tResampled blocks of joint currency/gold returns, preserving short-horizon dependence.\tThat the future resembles reordered history.\nParametric Gaussian\tMultivariate normal with the shrunk covariance of §6.2.\tBehavior under idealized, thin-tailed dynamics.\nFat-tailed\tMultivariate Student-t with heavy degrees of freedom.\tThat tails are Gaussian-sized.\nRegime-switching\tMarkov switching among the six regimes of §5.4 with stress transitions.\tThat regimes arrive gently and rarely.\nJump / crisis\tJump-diffusion paths with synchronized FX and gold jumps.\tThat moves are continuous.\nCorrelation collapse\tPaths with ρ_ij → 1.\tThat diversification persists when needed (§23.9).\nPurchasing-power drift\tPaths with persistent divergence between currency returns and the GPP index.\tThat purchasing-power tracking is easy.\n\nFor each family the program reports the dispersion of the weight trajectories, the distribution of turnover and execution cost, the drawdown distribution against the constitutional MDD envelope, and the reserve coverage distribution (§23.11). The stability requirement is not that W_t be constant — the architecture exists to let weights move — but that the distribution of outcomes remain inside the envelopes, the velocity limits and the cost budget across the path population. A model family whose behavior bifurcates under modest path variation is treated as non-robust by §23.7 regardless of its mean performance.\n\n§23.4 · LEAKAGE CONTROL\nPurged & Embargoed Validation\nbinding rule [MS §82, COO-24]\nThe binding rule\n“All model calibration, feature construction, optimizer parameter selection and ensemble weighting shall be performed without access to future test-period information. For overlapping data, leakage controls and purging shall be applied where required.” [COO-24]\n\nWalk-forward alone is not airtight when observations overlap: 756-day volatilities, long-window correlations, regime labels and ensemble performance scores can extend into or across the test period, quietly leaking future information into training. Four estimation targets receive the treatment explicitly — volatility, correlations, regimes, model ensemble weights [MS §82] — because their long windows make them the natural leakage channels.\n\nPurging + embargo\n\nPurging removes from the training set any observation whose label window overlaps the test window — a 756-day volatility estimate whose window reaches into the test period is dropped, not truncated:\n\nD_train^purged = { τ : [τ, τ+h_label) ∩ [t, t+h_test) = ∅ }\n\nEmbargo then deletes a further block of training observations immediately following the test window, because serial correlation lets information cross the boundary even without literal overlap:\n\nEmbargo: τ ∈ (t+h_test, t+h_test+h_embargo) excluded\n\nh_embargo is set at least as long as the longest autocorrelation in the return and signal series — by default the 30-day horizon — and is itself a published methodology parameter.\n\nWhy this matters enormously: MTQΣ is intended to govern a monetary reference methodology [COO-24]. A leaked validation record does not merely flatter a trading strategy — it silently changes what is claimed about a unit of account. Every out-of-sample number in the evidence base inherits its credibility from this section, and any methodology version that cannot demonstrate purge and embargo discipline in its validation logs is inadmissible at the gates of §23.12.\n\n§23.7 · SELECTION CRITERION\nThe Model-Selection Criterion\nstability, not return [MS §85]\n\nWhen several candidate models, parameterizations or methodology versions compete, the winner is not the highest return. Return is not the objective of a monetary reference; stability is. The decision function:\n\nScore = a(−Volatility) + b(−CVaR) + c(−MDD) + d(−PPError) + e(−Turnover) + f(−Cost) + g(Diversification) + h(Robustness)\nEVERY TERM ORIENTED SO HIGHER IS MORE STABLE\n\nVolatility, CVaR, maximum drawdown, purchasing-power error, turnover and cost enter negatively; diversification and robustness enter positively. Coefficients a–h are set by the Risk Council within constitutional envelopes, published with the methodology version, and themselves subject to the §23.6 perturbation study — so the ranking of models is not an artifact of the ranking weights.\n\nTWO TERMS DESERVE EMPHASIS\n\nThe drawdown coefficient c is explicitly non-zero because purchasing-power protection is experienced as drawdown, not as variance — a unit whose value never oscillates but occasionally drops 15% has failed its users even if its volatility statistics look excellent (§6.7). The robustness coefficient h aggregates the §23.6 perturbation sensitivities and §23.5 Monte Carlo dispersion, so a model that scores well only on the single realized history — the signature of overfitting — is penalized exactly where it is weakest.\n\nThe criterion operates at three levels: choosing among candidate models inside the ensemble (feeding the adaptive weights of §7.6), choosing the ensemble architecture itself, and promoting a new methodology version through the §23.12 gates. At every level the comparison uses the purged, walk-forward, out-of-sample record — never in-sample statistics, and never a single favorable window.\n\n§23.2 / §23.3 / §23.12 · HONEST STATUS\nRemaining Program vs Live Engine\ngates not executed\nAlready exercised by the reference engine\n✓\nThe S1–S11 deterministic scenario battery — Monte Carlo families, gold and currency stress, redemption runs, correlation collapse and parameter perturbation (§23.5–§23.11) — rendered live in the stress matrix above from /api/validation.\n✓\nCanonical risk state-machine tests — the 6-state machine with hysteresis, throttles and fee ladders.\n✓\nThe canonical invariant suite — constitutional invariants, MARP level logic, chain-link continuity, oracle pause/fallback discipline.\n✓\nDeterministic seeded generators — every scenario re-runs identically, path-for-path (§24.1), so results are reproducible.\nStill required by the full program\n•\nG1 (§23.12) — the 2010-start multi-horizon backtest (§23.2): full pipeline with costs, spanning the 2011 euro crisis, 2013 taper tantrum, 2015 franc depeg, 2020 COVID shock and the 2022–2023 inflation surge.\n•\nG2 (§23.12) — the concatenated walk-forward record (§23.3): expanding and rolling windows, varied re-estimation cadence, under §23.4 purging + embargo with zero leakage findings.\n•\nG4 (§23.12) — Risk-Council robustness bounds B_θ (§23.6): ΔPerformance ≤ B_θ for every registry parameter, not just the perturbation set the reference engine already runs.\n•\nGate execution §23.12 / §25.5: the ordered §23.12 evidence sequence (G1–G7) with independent reproduction, plus the 11 §25.5 production gates — none has been conducted; all report NOT PASSED.\n\nFailure at a gate does not end the program — it routes back to the implicated architecture layer, and the affected version re-enters validation.\n\nThe complete pilot evidence record — invariant test results, stress artifacts, audit findings and honest-status disclosures — is published in the Evidence Package.\n\nOpen Evidence Package\nSINGLE SOURCE OF TRUTH V5.0 + EXT-1/2/3\nMandate Compliance — Corrected Execution Layer\nRe-run self-tests\nTestnet activity: AUTHORIZED\nProduction reserve trading: NOT AUTHORIZED\nShadow mode: decisions recorded, funds never move\n\nThe corrected MARP execution layer (Listings C-1…C-10) runs in shadow mode: economic eligibility stays disabled until the Quantitative Committee approves the J(s) objective (OD-1) and the threshold policy (OD-2). "§26 · FINAL ARCHITECTURE\nFinal Declarations & Architecture Summary\nv2.0 consolidated · 2026-10-19\nTESTNET ONLY\nSHADOW MODE ENFORCED\nRESERVE TRADING NOT AUTHORIZED\n\n§1.7: testnet inspection, testnet-only corrected deployments, simulation and shadow-mode decision generation are authorized — production reserve trading is NOT.\n\n§26.1 · THE FINAL DECLARATION\nThe Final Declaration\n\nMTQΣ v2.0 is a closed-loop monetary architecture: every obligation the unit creates is defined, measured, collateralized, executed, verified and published inside one constitutionally governed loop. The declaration restates the whole architecture in one place — fixed-quantity references replaced by methodology references under invariant I3.\n\nv3.0 supersession note: the “collateralized” obligation above is v2.0 doctrine, reproduced verbatim. Under the v3.0 no-conventional-reserve objective (Gate B, D-13.3–D-13.7 open) MTQΣ is issued against settled payment and references the published measuring service — it is NOT collateralized by a conventional reserve; the legacy v1/v2 pilot-ledger path is retained, labeled.\n\nThe adaptive reference index — a methodology-defined, chain-linked basket of eligible currencies and gold — defines the reference value. [Ch 3 · Ch 9]\nThe Reserve NAV collateralizes the token. [Ch 14]\nMinting is priced against the reference index, breaking the 1:1 USD peg. [Ch 19]\nRedemption is priced against NAV and releases the full proportional basket. [Ch 19]\nThe weighting layer (MASE) computes live targets through a constrained, robust, multi-model ensemble. [Ch 5–7]\nThe execution layer (MARP) uses dynamic cost-benefit optimization with liquidity-aware sizing. [Ch 10–11]\nThe Buffer has three distinct layers (BASE, STRESS, EMERGENCY) with smooth ramping. [Ch 16]\nThe Oracle Architecture uses three independent sources with timestamp, confidence and deviation checks. [Ch 17]\nThe Geopolitical Eject system uses staged liquidation (10% → 25% → 50% → 100%) with a reintegration score. [Ch 21]\nThe Risk State Machine has six states (NORMAL, CAUTION, STRESS, DEFENSIVE, EMERGENCY, RECOVERY) with deterministic transitions. [Ch 18]\nAll 76 points of the independent monetary audit have been reconciled in the v1.2 lineage, and the v2.0 modification program closes the architectural gaps identified in the COO review. [§26.6.3]\n\nThis is not yet production-authorized. It is a candidate for testnet validation.\n\nThe reference methodology defines what one MTQΣ is intended to represent. The reserve portfolio exists to collateralize that obligation.\n\n§26.7 · THE FINAL ONE-SENTENCE DEFINITION\n\n“MTQΣ is a transparent, dynamically weighted, multi-currency-and-gold monetary reference architecture in which the live composition is continuously calculated by a constrained, robust, multi-model stability engine and changed only through cost-aware, risk-aware, liquidity-aware rebalancing rules.”\n\nEvery clause is load-bearing and traceable. If a summary of MTQΣ ever contradicts this sentence, the summary is wrong — the definition governs.\n\n§26.8.2 · FINAL HONEST STATUS DECLARATION\ncandidate for public testing — NOT production-authorized\nPRODUCTION-READY?\nNO — deployed only on testnet (Arc, Monad, Solana Devnet; Appendix D)\nORACLES\nMOCKED — all price feeds are simulated for testnet\nASSETS\nMOCKED — all ERC-20 assets are testnet mocks (except native USDC on Arc)\nLIVE WEIGHTS (V2.0)\nVALIDATION-STAGE — genesis snapshot equals the strategic prior; all subsequent targets are research outputs\nMAINNET DEPLOYMENT\nNOT PLANNED — pending the 11 validation gates (§25.5)\n§26.3.2 · COO-APPROVED FINAL ARCHITECTURE\nThe Two-Engine Hierarchy\nmerge at MARP\nMTQ Σ\nREFERENCE INDEX\nConstituency Engine\nEligible Universe\nMASE\nINSTITUTIONAL\nSTABILITY\nPURCHASING POWER\nRobust Ensemble\nConstraint Projection\nDynamic Bounds\nSmoothing\nDaily Target\nRESERVE\nReserve Engine\nCoverage / Liquidity\nCustody / Gold\n\nCoverage, liquidity, custody and gold state feed the cash-flow, no-trade-zone and risk-trigger inputs of MARP — execution is priced against reserve reality, not against the index alone (§26.3.3).\n\nMARP\nCash Flow\nNo-Trade Zone\nRisk Trigger\nCost-Benefit Gate\nPartial Execution\nVerification\nAudit / Publication\n\n§26.3.3 — the data-flow view (§26.3.1) and this hierarchy view are the same architecture from two angles: index mathematics and reserve economics run as parallel constitutional systems under one unit and converge only at MARP; downstream of MARP the cost-benefit gate, partial execution, verification, audit and publication are shared obligations of both engines.\n\n§26.2 · THE CRITICAL DISTINCTION\nFour Weight States\nthe most load-bearing distinction\n\nWPrior ≠ WTarget ≠ WSmooth ≠ WExecution\n\nStrategic Prior\nWPrior\n\nLong-term starting preference — a soft stabilizing anchor, never a target to be hit.\n\nTarget\nWTarget\n\nCurrent mathematical optimum, before constitutional constraints are applied.\n\nSmoothed\nWSmooth\n\nRisk-controlled transition toward the constrained target, paced by the stress-adaptive smoothing rule.\n\nExecution\nWExecution\n\nActual trade decision after cost, liquidity and reserve testing by MARP.\n\nThe published live weight WtLive is the Execution state that has actually been applied (§2.3; publication layer, Chapter 24). Every downstream system — index valuation (Ch 9), execution (Ch 10–11), publication (Ch 24) — consumes exactly one of these states; conflating them in any implementation is a specification defect.\n\nLIVE PILOT CROSS-REFERENCE (VALIDATION-STAGE)\ntargetGoldWeight 20.91%\nobservedGoldWeight 23.73%\n\nFrom the pilot snapshot — the deployed pilot’s simplified target/observed pair; never quoted as a final optimal weight (§26.6.2).\n\nAPPENDIX C · WORKED EXAMPLES\nFive Worked Examples — Scenario · Math · Outcome\nC.1\nno-trade zone\nNormal Operating Day (No-Trade Zone)\nSCENARIO\n\nMASE completes its daily calculation and posts a USD target of 28.2%, while the actual USD weight in the basket is 28.5%. Calm regime; the USD no-trade zone half-width is θ_USD = 1.0%.\n\nMATH\nD = |W_USD^Target − W_USD^Actual| = |28.2% − 28.5%| = 0.3%\nD = 0.3% < θ_USD = 1.0% ⇒ Trade = 0\nDECISION OUTCOME\n\nNo trade is executed. The deviation is recorded in the decision log with the trigger evaluation and the null trade; the actual weight carries forward. Daily calculation does not mean daily trading (I11).\n\nC.2\npartial rebalance\nModerate Deviation (Partial Rebalancing)\nSCENARIO\n\nMASE posts a gold target of 27.0% while the actual gold weight has drifted to 29.4% (gold appreciated against the currency components). θ_Gold = 2.0%; partial-rebalance coefficient β = 0.40.\n\nMATH\nD = |27.0% − 29.4%| = 2.4% > θ_Gold = 2.0% ⇒ trigger\nTrade = β·(W^Target − W^Actual) = 0.40 × (27.0% − 29.4%) = −0.96pp sold from gold\nPost-trade actual = 28.44% → residual deviation 1.44% (back inside the band)\nDECISION OUTCOME\n\nOnly a fraction of the deviation is corrected per trigger. If the deviation persists, consecutive daily triggers walk the basket toward the target in ~40% steps, each sized to remain liquidity-friendly.\n\nC.3\nnatural flow\nNatural-Flow Rebalancing\nSCENARIO\n\nA currency component is underweight and requires +1.5% of portfolio allocation. Before any external trade, the natural-flow test finds incoming mints and redemptions that can be directed into the component, contributing +0.9%.\n\nMATH\nTradeNeeded = 1.5% − 0.9% = 0.6% external\nDECISION OUTCOME\n\nOnly 0.6% requires external execution. Directed flows do the rebalancing work at zero marginal execution cost; the residual then passes through the same cost-benefit gate as Example C.4.\n\nC.4\ngate rejects\nCost-Gate Rejection\nSCENARIO\n\nA component is technically outside its preferred target, so a trade is eligible by the no-trade-zone test. The estimated benefit of the correction is B = 0.12% of portfolio value; the expected execution cost is C = 0.17%.\n\nMATH\nB − C = 0.12% − 0.17% = −0.05% < 0 ⇒ Trade = 0\nDECISION OUTCOME\n\nThe net value of the trade is negative, so the system refuses the value-destructive correction and carries the deviation, re-testing daily. Overrides are deliberate: a risk trigger can justify a solvency-protecting trade on risk grounds, and the emergency path supersedes normal gating entirely.\n\nC.5\nadaptive shift\nGold Adaptive Shift\nSCENARIO\n\nThe strategic prior for gold is W_Gold^Prior = 26%. On a given review MASE calculates a gold target of 29.2% — purchasing-power protection improved, diversification improved, crisis probability increased, and the marginal risk contribution of the additional gold remained acceptable.\n\nMATH\nW_Gold^Target = 29.2% ≠ W_Gold^Prior = 26%\nGold admissibility envelope 20–32% (§8.1): 29.2% is admissible\nThe StrategicDrift penalty (§7.1) already priced the 3.2-point departure inside the objective\nDECISION OUTCOME\n\nThe live target becomes 29.2%, not 26%. The executed path then runs through smoothing (§8.4) and the MARP gates of C.1–C.4 — fixed methodology, adaptive composition (§26.5.2).\n\n§26.6 · FINAL ARCHITECTURE STATUS\nLocked vs Must-Be-Validated\nconceptually complete · quantitatively unvalidated\nLocked concepts (constitutional)\n33\n1.\nMTQΣ is the project identity.\n2.\nGold is an explicit reference-basket component.\n3.\nGold is not merely a weighting multiplier.\n4.\nGold reference allocation and gold reserve allocation are separate.\n5.\nCurrency selection and currency weighting are separate.\n6.\nUSD/EUR/JPY/GBP/CNY/CHF are the initial core candidate universe.\n7.\nCAD/AUD/SGD and other currencies remain eligible candidates.\n8.\nStrategic percentages are priors, not fixed live weights.\n9.\nMASE is the adaptive weighting engine.\n10.\nMultiple optimizers are combined.\n11.\nRobust covariance is used.\n12.\nCVaR is included.\n13.\nPurchasing-power stability is included.\n14.\nRegime analysis is included.\n15.\nHard constraints exist.\n16.\nStrategic priors are soft anchors.\n17.\nTurnover is penalized.\n18.\nRebalancing is cost-aware.\n19.\nDaily calculation does not mean daily trading.\n20.\nNo-trade zones are used.\n21.\nBands can adapt.\n22.\nPartial rebalancing is preferred.\n23.\nNatural cash flow is preferred.\n24.\nReserve and index management are separate.\n25.\nMulti-source gold valuation is required.\n26.\nTokenized gold is an eligible instrument, not the definition of gold.\n27.\nNo artificial price support.\n28.\nNo discretionary buybacks.\n29.\nCrisis controls protect system integrity rather than market price.\n30.\nBacktests must be rebuilt for MTQΣ.\n31.\nWalk-forward and out-of-sample validation are mandatory.\n32.\nModel robustness matters more than maximum historical return.\n33.\nEvery live weight must be reproducible from published data and rules.\nNot locked — must be quantitatively validated\n14\n1.\nExact final currency weights.\n2.\nExact final gold weight.\n3.\nExact gold corridor.\n4.\nExact currency corridors.\n5.\nExact MASE model coefficients.\n6.\nExact ensemble coefficients.\n7.\nExact no-trade thresholds.\n8.\nExact smoothing factor.\n9.\nExact partial-rebalance coefficient.\n10.\nExact turnover penalty.\n11.\nExact crisis thresholds.\n12.\nExact reserve gold allocation.\n13.\nExact reserve liquidity buffer.\n14.\nExact review frequency where data quality suggests an alternative.\n\nNo numerical weighting percentage should be described as “the final optimal weight” until it survives the complete research program (Ch 23) and Gate 2 of the validation sequence (§25.5).\n\nAPPENDIX B · DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST\nDeployment Checklist — Phases PAR = 1.00 basket-unit, immutable (§2.5).\nW_t\nLive weight vector of the reference basket at time t; produced by the full MASE/MARP pipeline and published daily (Ch 24).\nW^Prior\nStrategic prior vector (USD 27 · EUR 20 · JPY 9 · GBP 8 · CNY 5 · CHF 5 · Gold 26 %); a soft anchor, deviation-penalized (§3.2).\nW^Target\nCurrent mathematical optimum before constitutional constraints are applied (Ch 7).\nW^Smooth\nRisk-controlled transition toward the constrained target, paced by stress-adaptive smoothing (§8.4).\nW^Execution\nActual trade decision after cost, liquidity and reserve testing by MARP (Ch 10–11).\nI_t (GFB reference index)\nUSD value of the chain-linked reference index; normalized to 1.00 at genesis (Ch 9).\nP_MTQ,t\nMint price of MTQΣ against the adaptive index: MTQ_minted = X_net / P_MTQ,t (Ch 19).\nNAV_t\nNet asset value of the reserve — V_net,t = Σ_j Q_j · P_j · (1 − H_j), net of haircuts; collateralizes the token (Ch 14).\nRR_t\nReserve ratio V_net,t / L_t; target 1.10, stress floor 1.05, hard floor 1.00.\nLCR_t\nLiquidity coverage ratio: liquid assets over stress-redemption demand; target ≥ 1.00 (Ch 13).\nQ_i\nComposite currency eligibility score of the Constituency Engine (six positive, two negative components) (§4.2).\nθ_i,t\nNo-trade zone half-width (band) for component i — the primary anti-churn mechanism (Ch 10).\nβ\nPartial-rebalance coefficient: executed size = β · (target − actual); C.2 uses β = 0.40 (Ch 10).\nρ_t\nStress-adaptive smoothing factor: W^Smooth = ρ_t·W_{t−1} + (1 − ρ_t)·W^Constrained (§8.4).\nα_m / η\nAdaptive ensemble coefficient of candidate model m (softmax over performance-stability scores), bounded by softmax temperature η (§7.6).\nMASE\nLayer 2 adaptive weighting engine (the stability engine): computes how much weight each eligible component receives (Ch 5–7).\nMARP\nLayer 3 execution engine: decides when, whether and how rebalancing trades execute — cost-aware, risk-aware, liquidity-aware (Ch 10–11).\nCOFER\nIMF Currency Composition of Official Foreign Exchange Reserves; the primary reserve-relevance input (§4.3.1).\nBIS\nBank for International Settlements; its Triennial Survey supplies FX turnover shares (§4.3.2).\nHysteresis\nEntry/exit threshold dead band (Q_entry > Q_exit) that prevents constituency oscillation (§4.7).\nEmbargo\nLeakage control (§23.4): deletes a block of training observations immediately after the test window; h_embargo ≥ the longest autocorrelation (default 30-day horizon).\nDivisor / chain-link (G_t)\nCumulative chain-link normalization factor that splices index history across weight or universe revisions; base fixings never change (Ch 9).\nDynamic buffer\nThree layers — BASE (62.5% gold), STRESS (85%), EMERGENCY (100%) — with 24-hour ramping and a first-loss waterfall (Ch 16).\nEject ladder\nStaged liquidation 10% → 25% → 50% → 100% minimizing market impact; re-admission requires reintegration score R ≥ 0.80 (Ch 21).\nAPPENDIX E · SOURCE LINEAGE\nSource Lineage Cross-Reference\n\nThe consolidated document is built from three sources: the Source of Truth Blueprint v1.2 (BP — 16 sections, 80 solidity blocks), the Consolidated Final Modification Specification Baseline v1.0 (MS — 103 sections), and the COO Review (COO-1…COO-28 plus the final conclusion). The hierarchy does not authorize a developer to invent an objective function, conversion factor, monetary threshold, protection margin, asset admission exception, reserve rule, feed address, emergency action, or purchasing-power benchmark — each such choice must be traced to an approved governance decision or remain explicitly blocked.\n\n§1.7 · STATUS DECLARATION (BINDS EVERY READER OF THIS SPECIFICATION)\n\nMTQΣ is a testnet-development and correction candidate. Testnet inspection, testnet-only corrected deployments, simulation, and shadow-mode decision generation are authorized within the boundaries of Appendix F. Production reserve trading is NOT authorized. No live-asset pilot is authorized unless Gate 5 has been completed and a separate written authorization is issued; production reserve trading additionally requires Gate 6 and a separate governance decision; Solana production and consolidated multichain operation require Gate 7. All values described as validation-stage values remain non-final. Reported chain deployments, addresses, token decimals, Safe settings, and role assignments must be verified independently. A written specification, a successful compile, a testnet transaction, or a simulation does not prove that a correction is deployed or that production is safe."

MARP (§10) then decides per-component whether the deviation between the smoothed target and the observed execution weight justifies a trade — with the v5.0 corrected-gate record attached in shadow mode. The published weight Wt is always theexecution weight (the §2.3 four-state rule: WPrior ≠ WTarget ≠ WSmooth ≠ WExecution).\n\nregime: Normal\nMARP total: $37.07K\nState badge: 4-state classifier (state decisions only) — the §5.4 six-regime probability model + CrisisScore are live below\n§6 / §7 · MASE CANDIDATE MODELS · §7.6 ADAPTIVE SOFTMAX ENSEMBLE\nPer-model weight vectors (WTarget candidates)\nadaptive α · 283 OOS samples\nη = 3 · e^(−η·Score) — lower score = greater weight\nModel\tUSD\tEUR\tJPY\tGBP\tCNY\tCHF\tGold\n\nMinimum Variance (§6.3)\nminvar · α=14.3%\n\t68.25%\t6.29%\t5.46%\t0.80%\t11.03%\t5.53%\t2.64%\n\nEqual Risk Contribution (§6.4)\nerc · α=14.3%\n\t36.82%\t11.34%\t10.34%\t9.50%\t13.26%\t10.17%\t8.56%\n\nMaximum Diversification (§6.5)\nmaxdiv · α=14.3%\n\t46.95%\t7.52%\t11.16%\t6.01%\t12.95%\t6.12%\t9.29%\n\nTail Risk · CVaR (§6.6)\ncvar · α=14.3%\n\t58.37%\t5.01%\t6.78%\t0.14%\t15.91%\t7.82%\t5.97%\n\nPurchasing-Power (§6.8 proxy)\nppp · α=14.3%\n\t30.59%\t21.25%\t10.71%\t8.47%\t5.29%\t5.27%\t18.42%\n\nInstitutional\ninstitutional · α=14.3%\n\t27.01%\t20.01%\t9.45%\t8.00%\t4.50%\t5.00%\t26.01%\n\nRegime-Adaptive (§5.4 mixture)\nregime · α=14.3%\n\t27.07%\t19.24%\t8.88%\t7.34%\t4.15%\t5.12%\t28.22%\n\nEnsemble Target\nWTarget · pre-envelope\n\t40.42%\t13.64%\t9.15%\t5.85%\t9.16%\t6.20%\t15.59%\n\nEach cell shows that model's recommended weight for the component; α is the §7.6 softmax weight from walk-forward OOS scoring (models are scored on their PREVIOUS proposals against realized returns — leakage-controlled by construction). "THE ASK · 6 PANELS\nPitch Deck — Closed-Loop Monetary Architecture\nv1.2 · FINAL · legacy\n\nThe Adaptive Reference Basket defines what one MTQΣ references. Under the v3.0 no-conventional-reserve objective (Gate B, D-13.3–D-13.7 open) there is no reserve portfolio — the legacy v1/v2 pilot-ledger path is retained, labeled. Designed for Sharia review (interest-free, asset-referencing, non-speculative). Candidate for public testing — NOT production-authorized. The six panels below are the full investor pitch — Problem, Solution, Market, Business Model, Traction, and The Ask — written in the brand voice: honest, sovereign, asset-referencing, calm.\n\n01 · PROBLEM\nStablecoins drift. Gold is illiquid. CBDCs are sovereign.\n·\nExisting stablecoins peg to a single fiat currency, exposing holders to that currency's inflation and political risk. Algorithmic stables have repeatedly depegged and collapsed.\n·\nTokenized gold is liquid only within its issuer's rails. CBDCs are sovereign instruments, programmable by the issuing state — not a neutral unit of global purchasing power.\n·\nThere is no on-chain unit that is asset-referencing, multi-currency, oracle-priced, and governed by a transparent constitution.\n02 · SOLUTION\nMTQΣ — a basket-referenced Global Digital Asset.\n·\nMTQΣ references a chain-linked, gold-anchored basket of seven components, seeded by a strategic prior (USD 27 / EUR 20 / JPY 9 / GBP 8 / CNY 5 / CHF 5 / Gold 26) with ADAPTIVE weights governed by admissibility envelopes (§8.1). One MTQΣ is intended to track that basket's purchasing power.\n·\nNo conventional reserve backs MTQΣ: under the v3.0 no-conventional-reserve objective (Gate B, D-13.3–D-13.7 open) the published index defines what 1 MTQΣ references. The legacy v1/v2 pilot-ledger path retains the Reserve-Ratio mechanics (110% §4.2 pilot target) as a labeled legacy exhibit — and no external audit exists (G1: internal audit only).\n·\nA 6-state risk machine, a 4-tier governance hierarchy, staged geopolitical eject, and a dynamic buffer protect the reference value under stress. Every parameter is published and timelocked.\n03 · MARKET\nA neutral unit for global settlement, savings, and reserves.\n·\nMarket context (industry estimates — no live source is quoted on this site): stablecoin supply was estimated to cross $200B in 2025; tokenized gold at an estimated ~$1.2B — fragmented and single-issuer. Institutional demand for an asset-referencing, multi-currency unit is unmet.\n·\nCross-border payments, treasury reserves for DAOs, and Sharia-compliant savings each need a unit that is asset-referencing, interest-free, and non-speculative.\n·\nMTQΣ is designed for Sharia review (interest-free, asset-referencing, non-speculative), opening a market segment largely unaddressed by existing stablecoins.\n04 · BUSINESS MODEL\nMint + redeem fees + pilot-ledger yield (legacy v1/v2).\n·\nMint fee 0.10% (§3.4). Redeem fee 0.15% (§3.4). Fees accrue to the protocol treasury.\n·\nOn the legacy v1/v2 pilot-ledger path, the reserve portfolio holds yield-bearing stablecoins + tokenized gold; net yield (post-haircut) accrues to the protocol, not to MTQΣ holders (MTQΣ is non-yield-bearing by design). The v3.0 no-conventional-reserve objective supersedes this path (Gate B open).\n·\nTreasury sweep (§13.2) moves surplus hot-wallet balance to a 4/7 Multi-Sig cold treasury above a $10K threshold, separating operational flow from long-term reserves.\n05 · TRACTION\nLive on 4 testnets · 10,300 Monte Carlo runs · audit trail in SQLite.\n·\nCanonical MTQΣ deployed across 4 testnets (Monad, Arc, Robinhood, Solana). Every chain has verified bytecode + role assignments.\n·\n10,300 Monte Carlo survival runs across 8 stress suites (Baseline, Monte Carlo, Depression, Hyperinflation, Depeg Cascade, Oracle Failure, Liquidity Crisis, Black Swan). Audit verdict: PASS.\n·\nLive pilot: every mint/redeem is logged to SQLite with full input/output, Reference Index, Reference Value, NAV (USD reporting), RR, LCR, and pass/fail reason — full audit trail via /api/trials/export.\n06 · THE ASK\nIndependent Sharia review + institutional pilot partners.\n·\nWe are seeking (1) an independent Sharia review board to certify the design as interest-free, asset-referencing, and non-speculative — closing the F4 informational finding honestly.\n·\n(2) Institutional pilot partners to deploy reserve collateral under the §5 registry criteria (issuer authorization, redemption right, custody, sanctions, audit, liquidity, oracle, concentration).\n·\n(3) Security auditors for the on-chain contracts (currently live on testnet — see the Security section). The protocol is candidate for public testing, not production-authorized.\nRead the full blueprint · verify on-chain\n\nThe pitch above is a summary. The blueprint reference, live on-chain verification, and the 10,300-run Monte Carlo audit are all available on this site — start with the Investor Verification section.\n\nInvestor Verification\nBlueprint Docs\nTest Audit"
